PA
Paul
2015-01-14 23:16 UTC
#75

There is a planned end to SotM (and to Tactics!). The former is Soon, but not Yet.

Also, to answer some questions from earlier, the reason we are doing a single preorder period for all of these items is that it allows customers the greatest possible flexibility when it comes to paying shipping.

If you want to minimize shipping costs, you can order any number of preorder items together in a single order and only pay shipping once. This entire order will be held until all of the preorder items are available. We also encourage people to combine pre-orders with friends; every copy of VotM or Broken City in a pre-order will come with a set of all promo items.

If you want to ensure that you receive preorder items as soon as they are released and do not mind paying extra shipping, you can place multiple orders, each containing different preorder items. For anyone interested in doing so, the items available through this preorder will be shipped in three “batches”, which are, not necessarily in chronological order:

  1. Villains of the Multiverse + Oversized Villain Cards

  2. Sentinel Miniatures: Minions + Sentinel Tactics maps

  3. Sentinel Tactics: Broken City + For Profit + Associated Miniatures

Furthermore, we *should* be able to have flat rate shipping for both Canada and the EU, with the later being at least a bit cheaper than it has been on the past on Kickstarter campaigns.

CH
Chaosmancer
2015-01-14 23:19 UTC
#78

And thus I'm broke before I even start getting money back…

 

But time for speculation and theories

 

First thoughts: I was going to mention this somewhere else, but we did see in Sky-scrapers deck a Plague Rat look-alike attached to a Bane style venom system. The reason I say look-alike is because I noticed some key differences. Mostly his pants which you can't see in the Villain box art. It's sky-scraper's Neutralizing Resonator and it shows plague rat with non-torn green and gray shorts, while normal plague rat has torn blue shorts. The fur is also lighter than the normal plague rat. Some theories as to why these differences. 1st: Someone created a second plague rat by forcefully injecting a test subject. 2nd:  Plague Rat is similiar to the Lizard, using a special compound to change and therefore when the heroes beat him and cut him off he searched for a way to regain his "power". (blown slightly out of the water by double checking wiki) Wiki says the change was gradual and caused by the dealers drugs and pike's chemicals in the sewers, but still possible if the former drug dealer was crazed enough to want to become plague rat again. 3rd: Someone tried to make Plague Rat easier to command and more powerful a foe against the heroes.

 

Lady with the half-face and lady in the red could potentially be the two figures from Tachyon's incap side. Lady with the face seems very likely to be ice based and red lady could have wind powers.

 

We haven't seen the blue guy with the gun and the pompadour, but my guess is that he'll be a Lobo pastiche.

 

Saber-tooth looking guy is wearing an amulet, probably very important.

McBehrer
2015-01-20 04:18 UTC
#232

The thing is, the villains’appearances fit their names. If you had nothing but their pictures to go off of, I think it’s pretty reasonable that you could easily match up the names with the pictures. Baron Blade? Gotta be the mad scientist guy. Grand Warlord Voss? Citizen Dawn? Easy. Fright Train? Friction? Proletariat? GET OUTTA HERE!

I can see some of them getting mixed around, like Omnitron/Infinitor, maybe Ambuscade/The Operative, or Deadline/Progeny, but that’s only with thematically similar names. A name like Sergeant Steel has to go on a tough, gritty, buff military type. It HAS to. Biomancer has to be a creepy scientist/alchemist/necromancer thing. The Sabertooth lookalike is probably not going to be something like “Overseer” or “Mind Bender;” it will be something feral and vicious, to reflect his appearance, which reflects his personality, which reflects his play style. That’s just how design works; it has to fit readily, or it presents a cognitive disconnect for the player that is detrimental to the overall impact of the characters and their stories.

Edit to respond to the above: sure, military women can dress up off duty, but being in a villain group is hardly off-duty. She could easily have been Sergeant Steel, but in order to do so they would have made her look the part, as described in this post. As is, she will likely have a name related to her stone/ice powers, or perhaps a position of authority, due to her VERY fancy getup. Maybe something like the Cold Countess, or something less stupid, since I’m crap at coming up with good names.

For the villain mini-expansion, I'm hoping that we get:

The short yellow, beaky person on Rook city Wraith's incap (here).  IF we are following the batman-esque tropes, we are missing a Penguin archetype, so my hope is that the yellow one is a fence who sends out supervillain thieves (such as the "For Profit" crew) to acquire valuable items for him/her.

It seems plausible that they might star in a mini-expansion, either as Vengeance style villains, or in the deck of a full villain who prefers to remain at arms length.  

 

For the very final villain of the game, I've been wondering if they might do something funky like having the villain (IE, Terminus) be an entire playmat, with different spaces for the heroes depending on if their were 3,4, or 5 of them, and a villain along with- so your team would be fighting a villain inside a massive world-destroying thing to see if the good guys or the bad guys would be able to dictate the fate of the multiverse.  After all- this is a being that (presumably) has the power to alter space, time, and reality itself.  Can you imagine a more tempting prize?  Think of what someone like Citizen Dawn could do with that- she could wipe out all the normals in one go!  Baron Blade would finally have his vengeance!  Infinitor could finally make the voices (ALL the voices) stop forever.

You could have a playmat that altered the rules and had objectives.  Terminus could be a villain in and of itself (that you played on the playmat representing it's world-ending machine), or it could be a place for the villains to try and control, so that they could alter time and space to their benefit...

My guess is that Revocorp serves as a front for a lot of villainous activity.  I'm guessing that Revocorp will supply any supervillain with:

1. Top-notch research facilities, custom designed.

2. Equipment and supplies (chemicals and compounds)

3. Research personnell that are as efficient as they are sociopathic

4. IT and physical security

5. Total confidentiality

What does Revocorp get out of this?

Well, I'm thinking that revocorp gets access to any material that is produced that the villain is willing to part with during their research.  Even things that Baron blade would consider failures could be invaluable for a clever company.  In teh event of the villain's capture, revocorp swoops in and cleans up anything that seems valuable, then torches the place (physical self-destructs in their equipment, computer viruses, chemical reactions, etc) so that their hands are clean.    

My guess is that Setback came to their attention when they supplied Blade with parts.  Then they sent Revenant to capture him.  Eventually, they succeeded (maybe when he had temporarily given up as a hero).  This might explain why Dark Watch Expat was on the roof of the Revocorp building- she was being overwatch for breaking Setback out.

BR
Braithwhite
2015-02-03 18:35 UTC
#407
Chaosmancer:

A few things 1st: confirmation that Revocorp is evil, finally!!! I do love the idea of them being middlemen instead of being out right owned by someone like Blade or the Chairman. The problem with their new strategy to go after Setback though is that this will totally blow their cover since the F5 know Blade was behind Setback's medical experiment. 

Actualy, I think that Revocorp is in the perfect position to maintain their cover, and play both ends against the middle. 

What do we actually know about them?  Let me pose what we know (and suspect) in two ways- a way that highlights their efforts as a "good" company, and a way that paints them in a negative light.

 

Positive:

  1. They contain dangerous physical specimens, like Plague Rat and Omnitron components.  They have rapid response teams to hunt down the mutations and meta-enhanced wildlife that are so often the byproducts of supervillains.

  2. They perform valuable chemical research to develop cures and means of controlling virulent diseases (like the Rat Plague).

  3. They develop new techniques in gene engineering, cybernetics, and specialized prostheses to help individuals born with ailments live full, healthy lives.

  4. Individuals with powers that they can't control, who are a danger to society can still live comfortable lives in their secure facilities while top-notch scientists try to find ways to help them control their abilities.  Setback is a case in point- the man has caused traffic pile-ups, and he's even tried to conspired with his thug friends to try and assassinate Revocorp's heroic CEO as he was testing his full-body prosthetic exosuit!  That suit was going to be used to cure orphans!  With diseases!

Revocorp!  Designing the future!

 

Negative:

  1. They fund supervillain thinktanks, so that they can reap the rewards of their mad brilliance.

  2. They help criminal empires, paramilitary groups, and private citizens gain powers through genetic manipulation, and cybernetics.

  3. They imprison superpowered people and creatures to exeriment on, or to control.

  4. They are accumulating relics of power, like Omnitron components, and experimenting on them without proper safeguards.

  5. They actively participate in criminal schemes and supervillain affairs, up to and including attempted murder.  After all, it was a Revocorp missile aimed at Setback in Wrong time and Place!

Revocorp!  Designing…The Future.

McBehrer

#408

So, this is what I can think of for Plague Rat:
He starts out with 4 handlers in play. These all have effects like healing him, increasing his damage to heroes, dealing damage themselves, and giving him DR. Maybe they have 7 or so HP each.

Plague Rat’s character card says, "at the end of the villain turn, play the top X cards of the villain deck, where X = 4 - the number of handlers in play.

These cards will be an assortment of one-shots that deal damage to EVERY target, one-shots that revive handlers, and ongoings that provide any variety of buffs and the like.

Advanced mode: damage dealt by Plague Rat to hero targets is increased by 1, or maybe made irreducible.

This represents his handlers guiding him towards attacking the heroes, keeping him alive, and fighting with him, while also reigning him in and keeping him from going berserk and damaging his allies.

I really like the Slaughterhouse Six from what I've seen.  Obviously they are an homage to Spider-Man's Sinister Six and I've noticed (as I'm sure have many others) that each original Sinister Six member (seemingly) has a direct analog in the Slaughterhouse Six:

 

Kraven/Ambuscade:  Big game hunters who get sick of hunting animals and turn to the "most dangerous game"

Manta Ray Guy/Dr. Octopus:  out-of-shape, sea animal-themed villains

Magma Guy/Sandman:  monstrous mineral creatures presumably with the minds of men

Electric Guy/Electro:  no explanation needed

Laser Bird Guy/Vulture:  no explanation needed

"Smoke and Mirrors"/Mysterio:  no explanation needed (and I'm officially predicting "Smoke and Mirrors" as her villain identity)

It's almost a shame that these ladies and gents will share one deck because it seems like they could be a threat all on their own.  The GtG guys really have a knack for putting a new and interesting spin on something safe and familiar.  I can't wait for this set.

I'm not sure how many EU customers the following applies to, but for me, in Ireland, the shipping isn't being calculated according to the EU rate, which is supposed to be $40 for orders under 10lbs, going up by $5 for every additional 10lb increment.  If I've understood correctly, I should be looking at a scale like this:
 
  • 0-10lbs: $40
  • 11-20lbs: $45
  • 21-30lbs: $50
  • 31-40lbs: $55
  • 41-50lbs: $60
  • 51-60lbs: $65
  • 61-70lbs: $70
  • 71-80lbs: $75
  • 81-90lbs: $80
  • 91-100lbs: $85
  • 101-110lbs: $90
  • 111-120lbs: $95
  • 121-130lbs: $100
 
However, when I put together a bunch of fake orders and brought them to the checkout to see what shipping totals would be generated, that's not what I saw.  Here is a list of shipping costs alongside the products being shipped.
 
  • For Profit: $29.23
  • Broken City & For Profit: $57.95 (additional charge for Broken City = $28.72)
  • Villains, Broken City, & For Profit: $64.08 (1 copy of Villains added to the order costs $6.13)
  • Villains x2, Broken City, & For Profit: $69.33 (1 copy of Villains added to the order costs $5.25)
  • Villains x3, Broken City, & For Profit: $78.09 (1 copy of Villains added to the order costs $8.76)
  • Villains x4, Broken City, & For Profit: $91.70 (1 copy of Villains added to the order costs $13.61)
 
This doesn't seem right.  I'd like to be able to generate some accurate shipping totals so that my friends and I can order together, so I hope the issue can be fixed during the pre-order period.  If I knew what all the pre-order products weighed, of course I could calculate the shipping myself and place an order with the warehouse directly, but I don't know that either.  Thanks in advance for any help.

To your point, could it be Gloomweaver related? Sure, and its not a bad alt theory. But the Zhu Long theory has a ton of art and story to back it up, so thats where my money's at.

#702

His henchmen little more than children...

 

I get that Pike grew "clones" for lack of a better word, but what I'm struggling with is why.

 

The Chairman in Sentinels was a mastermind, but his "power" was almost entirely reliant on Talent. The organization sought out individuals of talent to form a near impregnable power base. The Deputy, The Fence, The Muscle, The Contract, The Informant, The Operative. Pike relied on intelligent people, people who could understand how the whole relied on the parts.

 

Now he's growing superpowered infants to work for him. Why?

 

My best guess is that he feels he was betrayed. That's why his madness is taking this shape. Perhaps the Operative left Pike to save herself, as did many of his people. If he feels lack of loyalty caused his downfall raising individuals directly and ruling with an iron fist might be his next tack. He didn't fail he was let down and now that can never happen again.

RA
Rath
2015-02-24 20:58 UTC

Megalomania. Pike could not accept that the destruction of his organization was his own fault, so clearly it had to be the failings of his lackeys. Thus, the only solution is to create henchmen that were up to his standards.

Chaosmancer:

His henchmen little more than children… I get that Pike grew "clones" for lack of a better word, but what I'm struggling with is why. 

Check this passage out a little closer and I'll highlight what I think is relevant:

Exemplar holds himself to be the shining example of how man creates his own success. Hurdles and setbacks only exist to make the ultimate man even stronger. Graham Pike has left the trappings of the average person behind in exchange for perfection. Normal human morality and mortality are obsolete in the face of the works of a true visionary.
Why does anyone create clones? To try and create, and replicate, a perfect specimens. This bio draws Pike as obsessive about this type of perfection, and if he can achieve it through the drugs he has perfected since the SPITE experiements, it seems like a logical step toward recreating that success with his cloned minions.

Well, on the villain side:

We have Exemplar 

 

We have Spite, in the various drug-fueled incarnations. 

I'm guessing that it is a singular character with 5 different "modes" according to which drug he is using.  1 mind, 5 bodies. Possibly even the original spirit of spite possessing the clones. could be 5 different characters, with different powers.  

 

I'm guessing that there will be at least 1 other villain, making it comparable to the core game.  We've seen Revenant (at least I strongly suspect it is) lurking at the end of the core game, so I wouldn't be surprised to see Revocorp making a bid for control.  Given the Spite connection, Gloomweaver or a more mystical villain (possibly the cult of Gloomweaver? Will the cursed Acolyte finally be the villain we all know he is?) is also a strong possibility.  Another possibility would be remnants from whatever horrific event happened to break the city (aliens, demons, etc, etc).  

So that is 3-4 playable characters. 

 

On the hero side:

We have Dark Watch, with all 4 members.  that brings us to 7 (or 8 depending on the villains).  

 

Wildcards:

We have green energy girl, who I supect might be The Dreamer.  She had a signifigantly different upbringing from the Visionary, so I wouldn't be surprised if her powerset was substantially different.  Maybe reality alteration?  Could be a hero, could be a villain, could be both depending on how in control of her nightmares she is!

We have Nightmist, who is suspiciously absent.  I wouldn't be surprised to see her make an appearance, possibly as a wildcard- sometimes a villain when her curse is in control, sometimes a hero.  

Has Plague Rat broken Revocorp control and gone back to lurking in the shadows of Rook City? I wouldn't be surprised!

We know that Zhu Long makes a return at some point (given that Fixer is fighting him in a comic that mentions the dragon's return)- we could see Zhu Long's ninjas, or the Dragon himself (itself?).  This seems like a longshot to me, but a possibility nonetheless.  

Thiago wants to be a hero, and we know that he lived in Rook City. If any place needed a hero, Broken City is it.  We have a self-made madman- maybe we'll see a self-made hero leading the orphans of Broken City in response.  

 

Time will tell!

#824

Things to think about:

Each question had something to do with the reveal.  Ronway made a joke abut it in the earlier thread, but I think that it applies for each one.

Founding date of GtG revealed Citizen Hammer and Anvil: Adam and Christopher are frequently referred to as Citizen Hammer and Citizen Anvil (by themselves, by others, by me just now...)  So it certainly seems appropriate.

Anubis/Egyptian mythology with totemic significange (staff of Ra) revealed Bugbear: Bugbear carries a transformative totem (the bloodstone) that has some sort of influence and "hungers". Given the pyramid/mesoamerican focus, Aztec seems like a possibility for the mythology, which is even more interesting when taken in concert with Aztec sun-worship (which is where the human sacrifice comes in).  So the question is about one sun-worshipping culture's mythology, and led to the reveal of villain empowered by another sun-worshipping culture's mythology.  One has a staff, one has a stone that craves blood.

Villain who copies herself with illusions leads to Miss Information, a villain who is a cross-dimensional copy of Aminia Twain. One manipulates illusion, one manipulates reality. 

I'm going to skip biomancer for a second.

Cosmic connection leads to Greazer, who is an intergalactic bounty hunter with great hair.  It seems entirely plausible that he will have a connection to the mysterious cosmic entity- perhaps he is another Endling?  He certainly seems to be interested in capturing Captain Cosmic, who has a direct connection to the Cosmic Entity in the form of touching the shard.

KNYFE leads us to Sergeant Steel.  Which seems like a pretty obvious connection, given that they worked for the same organization.  I wonder if Paige was on that squad- We certainly know she has some battlefield experience with a crew.

 

So.  Biomancer.  the question was about Tachyon's wife, and the reveal was about someone who likes to manipulate flesh and replace people with doppelgangers.   

I'm starting to think that Tachyon has a very, very good reason for taking Biomancer on.  Lets hope that Dana survives.

Christopher has been saying that story is going to start coming together, and we've famously passed the point where instead of adding new things to speculate we're filling in the gaps.

So if this expansion is going to be doing that, then let's look at some major thematic players this expansion.

 

1.  Project Cocoon.

2.  Revo Corp

3.  Zhu Long

4.  Vampires and blood stones

#4 is a new one, but intrigues me on multiple levels.  But mostly because of the likelihood that it was in fact the unreached stretch goal for Infernal Relics, the expansion from which we gained Nightmist, Gloomweaver and Apostate.  The likelihood of a Fanatic Nemesis (and therefore an Apostate Nemesis) is high, and look at the colors in that image, red glowing windows, pale green light.  That's Gloomweaver territory.

Not that I think Gloomy is directly involved, but he could be, or it could be creatures fromt he same realm or using the same type of power.  Either way I think we may get some info filling in about the host and Gloomweaver and how they might interact.

If Bugbear is related to Gloomweaver even more so, stones that prolong life by absorbing your blood and life force (soul?) and Creatures believed to be Vampires?  This is getting good.

Revo Corp, Zhu Long, and the Vampires all have extended life themes going on, as does Biomancer and Bugbear.  That's a serious theme for this expansion, we've messed around in the wrong dark alleys, the powers beyond the veil, time itself and the forces of the cosmos.  This time we are messing around with the rules of life and death.

I can't wait to get some images and flavor text.

for Cyst- its not about "big and fat" its about managing his addiction TO eating everything.  Its about turning that food into toxic nastiness.  Its about being able to go right through stuff that other characters have to go around.  Its about heroes recoiling in horror at the maw under the mask.

BrotherDusk
2015-02-27 22:44 UTC
#943

I just logged in again after about a year of lurking to add my voice to phantaskippy's. I hope (but in all honesty don't beleive that) the Spites are either one set of 7 power cards that a "Spite Clone" player will interchange OR they are minion-status characters that the Exemplar can summon.  Below is my reasoning (please don't take my criticism as disparaging of >G or Christopher or Adam, my ultimate goal is to be constructive):

1.  The character design for a number of them is duplicative.  What can a rotund-large Spite do within the game's framework that is interesting enough and won't overlap or be duplicative of muscle-large Spite when divvied among seven power cards x5?  We already have a mind control character in Pinion, what other interesting things can Mindphyre do to fill a full moveset with the same theme and still be balanced?

2. The character design and story is lazy.  The five Spites fill stereotype roles of leader, bruiser, fatso, mage, and lifesyphon.  Frankly that's pretty boring and not all that related to the original Spite, who seemed to have about two modes.  First he is stealthy serial killer, then when you push him he turns into a monstrosity ala Akira. As an aside, THAT would make a great tactics character - someone who transforms at 1-2 remaining HP to absolutely wreck house, so as an opponent you want to overkill him dead rather than plink with small hits when he is at mid health and leave him a chance to transform.

3. The "types" filled by the Spites would be better spent on existing SOTM characters that haven't been given Tactics treatment and use up precious design and balance time that could be devoted to them.  I've mentioned the Mindphyre/Pinion analogy (if the character is just ranged-damage though, you could substitute any number of heroes or villians).  A full set of power cards for Karnal would probably be spot-on for Fright Train or Haka.  Demon Fist is "guy who punches" just as much as Fixer is OR if he has some ranged attacks, I think KNYFE would be a good candidate.  Cyst is somewhat unique, but if we are getting another big character, I'd rather it be Sky-Scraper.  Grudge seems to be dealing in infections/lifesap which used to be PlagueRat's role.  You get the picture.

 

AL
Arcanist_Lupus
2015-02-27 23:05 UTC
#944
BrotherDusk:

1.  The character design for a number of them is duplicative.  What can a rotund-large Spite do within the game's framework that is interesting enough and won't overlap or be duplicative of muscle-large Spite when divvied among seven power cards x5?  We already have a mind control character in Pinion, what other interesting things can Mindphyre do to fill a full moveset with the same theme and still be balanced?

We still don't have a full understanding of what Pinion can or can't do, or exactly how her or Mindphyre's powers work.  Furthermore, mind control is one of those powers who's possibilities expand hugely when you're not overburdened by ethics.  Tactics can easily hold 2 (or 3 if you count Visionary) psychic characters without be duplicative.

  1. The character design and story is lazy.  

 We've seen, what, a single picture and about two sentences describing each character?  Of course they feel a little flat.  Wait until we see more of them in Rook City

The five Spites fill stereotype roles of leader, bruiser, fatso, mage, and lifesyphon.  Frankly that's pretty boring and not all that related to the original Spite, who seemed to have about two modes.  First he is stealthy serial killer, then when you push him he turns into a monstrosity ala Akira. As an aside, THAT would make a great tactics character - someone who transforms at 1-2 remaining HP to absolutely wreck house, so as an opponent you want to overkill him dead rather than plink with small hits when he is at mid health and leave him a chance to transform.

I think that a lot people are forgetting that the Spites are individuals with their own personality.  I suspect that people expected them to be minions, and thus assumed that they would be flatter than main characters are, and are holding onto that assumption even with the reveal that they are fully fledged characters.  The fact that each one was given such a small personal bio doesn't help.  But these aren't mini-Spites - their powers have the same origin, but they have their own goals, tactics, and personalities.  They're not meant to replace Spite.

  1. The "types" filled by the Spites would be better spent on existing SOTM characters that haven't been given Tactics treatment and use up precious design and balance time that could be devoted to them.  I've mentioned the Mindphyre/Pinion analogy (if the character is just ranged-damage though, you could substitute any number of heroes or villians).  A full set of power cards for Karnal would probably be spot-on for Fright Train or Haka.  Demon Fist is "guy who punches" just as much as Fixer is OR if he has some ranged attacks, I think KNYFE would be a good candidate.  Cyst is somewhat unique, but if we are getting another big character, I'd rather it be Sky-Scraper.  Grudge seems to be dealing in infections/lifesap which used to be PlagueRat's role.  You get the picture. 

We currently have no projected endpoint of Tactics that I'm aware of.  As such, design and balance time are effectively infinite.  Design space is limited, but I think there's more of it than you realise.  Certainly enough for every character that >G intends to include.  And remember, >G are trying to tell a story.  Sure, Haka could fill the position of "big bruiser" mechanically, but he doesn't belong in Broken City.  Neither does Sky-Scraper.  Their time will come.  Just not yet.

I'm well aware many of my initial impressions of the Spites might be premature.  But at the same time I felt I needed to voice them because voicing them now is the only way they might gather support and affect the actual game before it releases.  Maybe >G will take a hard look at these characters and change things up (if I'm correct in my assumptions about how stereotyped and redundant they are).  They could even make changes without telling us at this point. 

As to how much story is left to develop for the Spites: The way some of >Gs characters are handled, this update and the resulting text might be all we ever know about the Spites. Usually they have one biography update and maybe a promo that shows us progression.  I think a lot of fans that are deep into SOTM don't remember that the vast majority of the canon is invented in their heads by drawing rough lines between a few solid images and a three paragraph story.

As for having infinite time for more Tactics, I'd argue that if this expansion falls flat enough, the tactics line could be winding down.  The current preorder campaign is at roughly 1/4 the popularity of the first box's kickstarter (I was among those).  I really want this product to be super cool, and I'm sorry expressing concerns isn't more of a fun topic!  ;)

I understand you, BrotherDusk. But I think that, if the Spites are "lacking", it will change during the play tests - "our" playtesters are very exigeant, as far as I know. I'd also point that, with Tactics, the scenarios you are given to play are more background story - they show you the actual adventures, goals and actions of the characters involved.

It happened a lot of times before - we read the Bios, maybe we see a base power and a few illustrations, and we feel something is lacking. I remember ChronoRanger being seen as "redundant" by some because he was a gun hero, as Expatriette, because nothing about his gameplay was revealed at the time. We tend to assume a lot about how a character will play / feel from what is only a preview, only scratching the surface, and sometimes written to deliberately mislead the reader.

I am not saying you should keep your concerns to yourself, I am just trying to assure you that it isn't a catastrophe in the making :D I am sorry if I may sound patronizing - it is not my intent, what I mean is that I trust the "GtG + their playtesters" combo a lot about good gameplay and good stories.

This said, Rook/Broken City, in both incarnations, is a more gritty take on superheroics, and it is less appealing to the audience, in my opinion. The dark themes conflict a lot with what most expect from superheroes nowaday. Especially if you find yourself playing vile characters rather than heroes.

As for the popularity of Tactics, the main reason I resolved not to invest in them is that my fellow gamers are not as interested with super-hero antics as I am (and less fluent in English), so I mostly play SotM solo, something that is less interesting adn easy to do with Tactics. It's in fact the only thing that keeps me from jumping in the boat, as I have no sense of money (and I am counting my beans, right now, because I have a hard time resisting).

Tegre
2015-02-28 17:07 UTC
#960

I don't think Tactics will wind down as fast, its still in its growing pains.  its getting one fan base to adopt a new style of game and play that the originating game captured.

Sentinels of the Multiverse will probably looked at as the game that caught the feel of being a super hero in league with other heroes who are all special and different in their own way.  Because it blends so many of our favorite comicbook characters, its why we all keep coming back to it, its also why we get excited when a new hero comes out in a box set because we try to find an analoge of a hero we all love (or loathe.)  

Tactics is trying to do the same thing, but I wonder if part of the problem is the simplicty of how it does it.  Is there something that could be done that makes or improve on Tactics? Was it too rushed out the door and it could use something else? Is it simply the fact that we really can only enjoy it with at least one other person, where as with SotM we can enjoy solo?  What magic is Tactics missing that SotM captured and kept us going back?

But back to the point I wanted to make.  Should >G still continue with the tactics line, I think it will follow the same pattern as SotM.  Which was Base Game >Rook City (Enhanced Humans)>Infernal Relics (Magic Set)>Shattered Timelines>(Time Travelers)>Vengeance(Villian!)>Wrath of the Cosmos (Cosmic Set)>Villians of the Multiverse (More Villains!)>Final Exapansion (Biggest bads of all)

The one thing I DO like most about tactics is also what related to what I don't like about tactics.  While it seems we get a Base set, we also get an expansion.  So while (so far) it follows the same "timeline" above, the expansion gives us more characters in the multiverse.  However...Do we need a base set every time? - Sadly yes, since each base set really is more modualr terrain in a different environment, plus what we would get in each expansion (that we're looking forward to) - More heroes, more villains and more story.

So Technically after Broken City, I'm expecting to see Argent Adept and almost all of the Prime Wardens except Captain Cosmic.  If we DO see Captain Cosmic, then I expect we'll see an alternative panel much later.  Gloomweaver, Apostate and an Oversized Villain Akash'bhuta, we'll have The Realm of Discord on one side and maybe a giant pyramid interior for the other - The expansion will be The NEW Ennead and Anubis.  As a twist they may introduce a new "god" character.

As far as Nightmist goes...I don't know the full extent of how much she is a >G character versus a >G and Richard Launius character.  which could be why she is currently "lost in time and space" We know she is at least communicating with Pinion - but I'm sure one of the >G guys can confirm how much use they can getout of her.

#961

A couple of things about Tactics.  One, I think it's important to remember that, being a very different type of game, it appeals to a different audience than SotM.  At PAX South, for example, Tactics was very popular and being demo'd consistently and all of the teams that played in the tournament learned the game there, which speaks to it's ease of entry.  GtG has says they are committed to growing Tatics and know it does not have the same initial base as a SotM expansion so I wouldn't get worried about it winding down just yet.

As much as I love SotM (it is my favorite game), Tactics does the best job of any game I've ever played of truly capturing what it feels like to be that hero.  Tachyon doesn't rely on card mechanics to simiulate speed, she literally runs acorss the entire map in one turn.  Ambuscade is a sneaky hunter, Bunker is a slow moving armory - these are things that you don't feel as much in the card game and one of the great strengths of this game.

As for Base sets, Tactics appears to be going by teams so I would expect the next set to be Prime Wardens focused (including CC).

phantaskippy:

I prefer style and realistic appearances to the standard sexualization, and with a lot of people here that is part of the initial attraction of SotM.

Yep. This is particularly relevant to me as a female gamer, since it's often the case that there are no or few female characters in games and if there are they are usually exclusively cheesecake-style, wearing clothing that's wildly inappropriate for the job they're supposed to be doing. I'm good with tight and/or revealing clothing or even nudity, but not if you're off to fight a dragon. It's really tiring to see that all the damn time - women aren't "proper" characters, they are just there to look hot. Or they don't exist. And they all look the same.

So when we got the base Sentinels it was really refreshing to see four female heroes (and one "other") out of the ten, with none in ridiculous backbreaking poses and all in clothing that isn't the superhero equivalent of the chainmail bikini (okay, so Visionary's is pushing it a little for some people, but it's not so bad). I'd really hate for Sentinel Comics to lose that more inclusive flavour.

And so to game artists in general, here are my requests:

  • More variety in male and female bodies please, not just skinny-with-huge-boobs females and huge beefcake men.

  • Dress characters appropriately for their role/job/the weather.

  • Don't make anyone sexy just for the sake of being sexy. Sexy is fine, but as above, have them behaving and posing in a way that's appropriate for the scene, and their character. No Playboy poses during dragon fights.

  • And for goodness sake, WOMEN NEED STRAPS!

I don’t think anybody is hating on characters, Adam, or the game. We also aren’t remarking that this is in any way a sudden change; we’re just concerned that the art has drifted over time from refreshingly different towards disappointingly generic and has reached a point where people feel moved to comment. As an aside, I really don’t understand why anyone thinks the typical objectified way women are portrayed in superhero and fantasy art is in any way healthy, normal, or acceptable. It’s crass, immature, and ultimately just terrible art.
I know Adam doesn’t post much but I’d really like to know what his thoughts are on how his style has matured and developed over the past few years, and what he thinks of the points raised here.

SI
Silverleaf
2015-03-01 11:35 UTC
#991
There's a big difference between "sexuality" and "sexualisation".
 
And I'm commenting on female characters in general, not just the villains.
 
I have no problem with the female body as long as it looks realistic and is appropriate for the role. And nudity or partial nudity or tight clothing is fine too - there's nothing shameful in the human form and I think as a society it's about time we got over this idea that female bodies especially are inherently sexual and therefore "dirty".
 
But again, I come back to this word "appropriate". Miss Information's new dress is pretty deliberately sexy on her part and probably not terribly functional - I assume there's a reason for that which will be made clear, and if she's not physically fighting then that's fair enough. I'd be complaining if she was wearing that (or the standard spandex) in her FV assistant role, because it's not work appropriate.
 
I hate Tactics Wraith wearing heels to run around fighting villains, for similar reasons. Not appropriate, because she's far more likely to injure herself and you simply can't run as fast in heels. Heels are primarily worn to make you look taller and/or sexier, and there's no need for Wraith to care about either of those things when she's trying to get a job done, especially with the added disadvantage of reduced mobility. Not appropriate.
 
And where on earth did Mind-Phyre get a costume that's obviously tailored especially for her, if Rook City's been practically destroyed and Exemplar has to wear a suit made of rags?
 
Props to Parse though. She looks comfortable and not restricted by her clothing. But it's interesting that the most "comfortable" character (apart from the Scholar) has Asperger's, presumably with the common symptom of sensitivity to touch meaning she wouldn't be able to cope with a tight costume, while the "normals" almost all wear them. She's wearing "normal" clothing because she's "not normal".
 
My second complaint is the cookie-cutter approach to bodies. The message to me is "This is the only type of body that is allowed to be a superhero, and this is the only type that is attractive." I'm sure Adam doesn't mean to send that message, but that's the message nonetheless. And it's the same "idealised" body shapes we're constantly being bombarded with from other media. As a woman I'm tired of those relentless pretty identical images that are held up to us not only as ideals, but as the only possible option.
 
I know this is very much atypical, but I know a woman who is about 5'6" and maybe 200lb. She's broad-shouldered and muscular, but she's clearly overweight and could stand to lose 40lb to reach a "normal" weight. I met her at the gym. She was leading an aerobics class I joined - I mean PROPERLY leading, as she did everything the class did and more. By the end of the hour she'd hardly broken a sweat (I was exhausted, it was a tough class), and she lead FIVE different aerobics classes every day. So big and fit? Totally possible. Slender and fit? Check out Olypic heptathletes or decathletes. Muscular and fit? Wrestlers, or hammer throwers, or sprinters.
 
Not just one " ideal" shape.

Yep. Again I'll reference my fat aerobics instructor, who was definitely overweight but able to do five hours of cardio classes a day plus some strength training. She was a big woman with fairly evenly-distributed body fat, but she was way fitter than most of the skinny women who took her classes.

10 years ago I was doing kung fu training three times a week with regular sparring, and going to the gym for 2 hours on two other days. The other two days I usually walked for 2 hours, and I worked behind a bar in a busy nightclub 12 hours a week (and if you don't think that's exercise you've never tried it). I weighted 210lb when I started, at 5'6", and I began eating sensibly as well. I've never been much of a drinker.

In a year I lost about 20lb. I was still fat. I still am.

Saying that though, there's no way I could look like a standard comic book female, even if I had no body fat. My shoulders and rib cage are too wide, my thighs are too muscular, I couldn't achieve the hourglass shape because my waist is also very muscular. A thin me would look square, and broader than Sky-Scraper pretty much everywhere except the upper arms (I'm too sick to do very much physically now, definitely no weights).

Most female comic book characters are narrower than it's possible for me to be, even if I was starving and had absorbed my own muscle to survive.

It's a good job I'm secure in my shape, that's all I can say.
